            return np.zeros_like(self.contributions)

        return self.contributions / total

    def top_features(
        self,
        feature_names: list[str],
        k: int = 10,
    ) -> list[tuple[str, float]]:

        importance = self.normalized

        indices = np.argsort(-importance)[:k]

        return [
            (
                feature_names[i],
                float(importance[i]),
            )
            for i in indices
        ]


class ExIFFI:
    """
    ExIFFI implementation specialized to the standard
    axis-aligned Isolation Forest.

    For a standard Isolation Forest node:

        f(x) = x_j - tau

    and therefore the split-direction vector is:

        v = e_j

    Hence the node-level feature attribution is assigned
    entirely to feature j.

    The path weighting follows the ExIFFI formulation:

        lambda_k(x) = |X_k| / |X_{k+1}|

    for standard Isolation Forest because ||v|| = 1.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        model: IsolationForest,
        feature_names: list[str],
    ) -> None:

        if not hasattr(model, "estimators_"):
            raise ValueError(
                "IsolationForest must be fitted before "
                "creating ExIFFI."
            )

        if len(feature_names) != model.n_features_in_:
            raise ValueError(
                "feature_names length does not match "
                "model.n_features_in_."
            )

        self.model = model
        self.feature_names = feature_names
        self.n_features = model.n_features_in_

    def _as_model_frame(
        self,
        x: np.ndarray,
    ) -> pd.DataFrame | np.ndarray:

        if isinstance(x, pd.DataFrame):
            return x

        x_array = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)

        if x_array.ndim == 1:
            x_array = x_array.reshape(1, -1)

        if hasattr(self.model, "feature_names_in_"):
            return pd.DataFrame(
                x_array,
                columns=list(self.model.feature_names_in_),
            )

        return x_array

    def _get_path_nodes(
        self,
        estimator,
        x: np.ndarray,
    ) -> np.ndarray:

        indicator = estimator.decision_path(x)

        start = indicator.indptr[0]
        end = indicator.indptr[1]

        return indicator.indices[start:end]

    def _node_information(
        self,
        tree,
        node_id: int,
        x: np.ndarray,
    ) -> tuple[int | None, float]:

        feature = tree.feature[node_id]

        # Leaf node.
        if feature < 0:
            return None, 0.0

        left = tree.children_left[node_id]
        right = tree.children_right[node_id]

        n_parent = tree.n_node_samples[node_id]

        threshold = tree.threshold[node_id]

        if x[feature] <= threshold:

            child = left

        else:

            child = right

        n_child = tree.n_node_samples[child]

        if n_child <= 0:
            return feature, 0.0

        # ExIFFI node/path weight.
        #
        # Standard IF uses an axis-aligned split,
        # therefore ||v|| = 1.
        weight = (
            n_parent / n_child
        )

        return feature, float(weight)

    def explain(
        self,
        x: np.ndarray,
        sample_index: int = 0,
    ) -> ExIFFIExplanation:

        x = np.asarray(
            x,
            dtype=float,
        )

        if x.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(
                "x must be one-dimensional."
            )

        if len(x) != self.n_features:
            raise ValueError(
                "Observation dimensionality does not "
                "match the fitted model."
            )

        x_2d = x.reshape(1, -1)
        x_model = self._as_model_frame(x_2d)

        raw_importance = np.zeros(
            self.n_features,
            dtype=float,
        )

        for estimator in self.model.estimators_:

            tree = estimator.tree_

            path_nodes = self._get_path_nodes(
                estimator,
                x_2d,
            )

            for node_id in path_nodes:

                feature, weight = (
                    self._node_information(
                        tree,
                        int(node_id),
                        x,
                    )
                )

                if feature is None:
                    continue

                raw_importance[feature] += weight

        # Preserve sklearn's score_samples convention.
        #
        # More negative values correspond to more
        # anomalous observations.
        anomaly_score = float(
            self.model.score_samples(x_model)[0]
        )

        return ExIFFIExplanation(
            sample_index=sample_index,
            anomaly_score=anomaly_score,
            contributions=raw_importance,
        )

    def explain_batch(
        self,
        X: np.ndarray,
    ) -> list[ExIFFIExplanation]:

        X = np.asarray(X, dtype=float)

        if X.ndim != 2:
            raise ValueError(
                "X must be two-dimensional."
            )

        return [
            self.explain(
                X[i],
                sample_index=i,
            )
            for i in range(len(X))
        ]

    def path_features(
        self,
        x: np.ndarray,
    ) -> dict:
        """
        Return the actual Isolation Forest decision-path
        features for one observation.

        Used for implementation validation and research
        diagnostics.
        """

        x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)

        if x.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError("x must be one-dimensional.")

        x_2d = x.reshape(1, -1)

        result = {}

        for tree_index, estimator in enumerate(
            self.model.estimators_
        ):

            tree = estimator.tree_

            path_nodes = self._get_path_nodes(
                estimator,
                x_2d,
            )

            features = []

            for node_id in path_nodes:

                feature = tree.feature[
                    int(node_id)
                ]

                # Ignore leaf.
                if feature < 0:
                    continue

                features.append(
                    int(feature)
                )

            result[tree_index] = features

        return result

=== FILE: ./src/efd/explanations/base.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod

import numpy as np

from efd.explanations.types import (
    LocalExplanation,
)


class ExplanationMethod(ABC):
    """
    Common interface for model explanation methods.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    def explain(
        self,
        x: np.ndarray,
        sample_index: int = 0,
    ) -> LocalExplanation:
        """
        Explain one model decision.
        """
        raise NotImplementedError

    @abstractmethod
    def explain_batch(
        self,
        x: np.ndarray,
    ) -> list[LocalExplanation]:
        """
        Explain multiple model decisions.
        """
        raise NotImplementedError
=== FILE: ./src/efd/explanations/validation.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np


def rank_features(
    contributions: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Rank features by absolute contribution.
    """

    contributions = np.asarray(
        contributions
    )

    return np.argsort(
        -np.abs(contributions)
    )


def top_k_features(
    contributions: np.ndarray,
    k: int,
) -> np.ndarray:

    ranking = rank_features(
        contributions
    )

    return ranking[:k]


def mask_features(
    x: np.ndarray,
    feature_indices: np.ndarray,
    baseline: float = 0.0,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Remove/neutralize selected features.

    The returned vector is a copy.
    """

    perturbed = np.asarray(
        x,
        dtype=float,
    ).copy()

    perturbed[
        feature_indices
    ] = baseline

    return perturbed
=== FILE: ./src/efd/experiments/static_if_exiffi.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from efd.data.preprocessing import (
    FeaturePolicy,
    NumericTransactionPreprocessor,
)

from efd.data.temporal import (
    chronological_windows,
    transaction_date,
)

from efd.models.isolation_forest import (
    IsolationForestDetector,
)

from efd.explanations.exiffi import (
    ExIFFI,
)


def run_experiment(
    transactions_path: str,
    output_dir: str,
    train_fraction: float = 0.60,
    n_future_windows: int = 4,
    n_estimators: int = 300,
    random_state: int = 42,
    explanation_samples: int = 100,
    reference_date: str = "2017-12-01",
):
    print("=" * 70)
    print("BASELINE 1 + EXIFFI")
    print("=" * 70)

   
    # 1. Load data

    print("\n[1/8] Loading transaction dataset...")

    df = pd.read_csv(
        transactions_path,
        low_memory=False,
    )

    print(
        f"Loaded {len(df):,} transactions "
        f"and {len(df.columns):,} columns."
    )

    # 2. Chronological split

    print("\n[2/8] Creating chronological split...")

    train, future_windows = chronological_windows(
        df,
        time_col="TransactionDT",
        train_fraction=train_fraction,
        n_future_windows=n_future_windows,
    )

    print(
        f"Training observations: {len(train):,}"
    )

    print(
        f"Future observations: "
        f"{sum(len(w) for w in future_windows):,}"
    )

    print(
        f"Future windows: {len(future_windows)}"
    )

    print("\nTraining temporal range:")

    print(
        f"  {transaction_date(train['TransactionDT'].min(), reference_date)}"
        f" → "
        f"{transaction_date(train['TransactionDT'].max(), reference_date)}"
    )

    # 3. Preprocessing

    print(
        "\n[3/8] Fitting preprocessing "
        "on training period only..."
    )

    preprocessor = NumericTransactionPreprocessor(
        policy=FeaturePolicy(
            target="isFraud",
            temporal="TransactionDT",
            identifier="TransactionID",
            max_missing_rate=0.95,
        )
    )

    x_train = preprocessor.fit_transform(train)

    feature_names = preprocessor.feature_names_

    print(
        f"Selected features: {len(feature_names)}"
    )

    print(
        f"Removed >95% missing: "
        f"{len(preprocessor.removed_high_missing_)}"
    )

    print(
        f"Removed constants: "
        f"{len(preprocessor.removed_constant_)}"
    )

    # 4. Train static Isolation Forest
    print(
        "\n[4/8] Training static Isolation Forest..."
    )

    detector = IsolationForestDetector(
        n_estimators=n_estimators,
        max_samples="auto",
        contamination="auto",
        random_state=random_state,
        n_jobs=-1,
    )

    detector.fit(x_train)

    print(
        f"Isolation Forest trained with "
        f"{n_estimators} trees."
    )

    
    # 5. Create ExIFFI explainer
    print(
        "\n[5/8] Initialising ExIFFI..."
    )

    explainer = ExIFFI(
        model=detector.model,
        feature_names=feature_names,
    )

    print(
        f"ExIFFI ready for "
        f"{len(feature_names)} features."
    )

    # 6. Explain Window 1 sample
    print(
        "\n[6/8] Generating Window 1 explanations..."
    )

    window = future_windows[0]

    x_window = preprocessor.transform(window)

    n_explain = min(
        explanation_samples,
        len(window),
    )

    x_sample = x_window[:n_explain]

    y_sample = (
        window["isFraud"]
        .iloc[:n_explain]
        .to_numpy()
    )

    scores = detector.anomaly_score(
        x_sample
    )

    explanations = explainer.explain_batch(
        x_sample
    )

    print(
        f"Explained {len(explanations):,} "
        f"observations."
    )

    # 7. Construct explanation output
    print(
        "\n[7/8] Building explanation dataset..."
    )

    output_dir = Path(output_dir)

    output_dir.mkdir(
        parents=True,
        exist_ok=True,
    )

    rows = []

    for i, explanation in enumerate(
        explanations
    ):

        row = {
            "sample_index": i,
            "isFraud": int(y_sample[i]),
            "anomaly_score": float(scores[i]),
        }

        importance = (
            explanation.normalized
        )

        # Preserve the complete explanation vector.
        for feature_index, feature_name in enumerate(
            feature_names
        ):
            row[
                f"importance__{feature_name}"
            ] = float(
                importance[feature_index]
            )

        # Top-10 features for convenient inspection.
        top_indices = np.argsort(
            -importance
        )[:10]

        for rank, feature_index in enumerate(
            top_indices,
            start=1,
        ):

            row[
                f"top_{rank}_feature"
            ] = feature_names[
                feature_index
            ]

            row[
                f"top_{rank}_importance"
            ] = float(
                importance[feature_index]
            )

        rows.append(row)

    explanation_df = pd.DataFrame(
        rows
    )

    explanation_path = (
        output_dir
        / "static_if_exiffi_w1_sample.csv"
    )

    explanation_df.to_csv(
        explanation_path,
        index=False,
    )

    # Save compact top-feature table

    top_rows = []

    for i, explanation in enumerate(
        explanations
    ):

        top_features = (
            explanation.top_features(
                feature_names,
                k=10,
            )
        )

        for rank, (
            feature,
            importance,
        ) in enumerate(
            top_features,
            start=1,
        ):

            top_rows.append(
                {
                    "sample_index": i,
                    "isFraud": int(
                        y_sample[i]
                    ),
                    "anomaly_score": float(
                        scores[i]
                    ),
                    "rank": rank,
                    "feature": feature,
                    "importance": importance,
                }
            )

    top_df = pd.DataFrame(
        top_rows
    )

    top_path = (
        output_dir
        / "static_if_exiffi_w1_top_features.csv"
    )

    top_df.to_csv(
        top_path,
        index=False,
    )

    # Save metadata
    metadata = [
        "BASELINE 1 + EXIFFI",
        "",
        f"Training observations: {len(train)}",
        f"Future windows: {len(future_windows)}",
        f"Window explained: 1",
        f"Observations explained: {n_explain}",
        f"Features: {len(feature_names)}",
        f"Trees: {n_estimators}",
        f"Random state: {random_state}",
        "",
        "ExIFFI:",
        "Standard Isolation Forest axis-aligned splits",
        "Node feature direction = selected feature axis",
        "Path weight = parent_node_samples / child_node_samples",
        "",
        "Outputs:",
        str(explanation_path),
        str(top_path),
    ]

    metadata_path = (
        output_dir
        / "static_if_exiffi_w1_metadata.txt"
    )

    metadata_path.write_text(
        "\n".join(metadata),
        encoding="utf-8",
    )


    # 8. Summary
    print(
        "\n[8/8] Experiment complete."
    )

    print(
        f"\nFull explanations:"
        f"\n{explanation_path}"
    )

    print(
        f"\nTop features:"
        f"\n{top_path}"
    )

    print(
        f"\nMetadata:"
        f"\n{metadata_path}"
    )

    print("\nFirst observations:")

    print(
        top_df.head(30).to_string(
            index=False
        )
    )

    return explanation_df


def main():

    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description=(
            "Baseline 1 + ExIFFI "
            "on IEEE-CIS"
        )
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--transactions",
        required=True,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--output-dir",
        default=(
            "results/explanations"
        ),
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--train-fraction",
        type=float,
        default=0.60,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--windows",
        type=int,
        default=4,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--trees",
        type=int,
        default=300,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--samples",
        type=int,
        default=100,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--random-state",
        type=int,
        default=42,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--reference-date",
        default="2017-12-01",
    )

    args = parser.parse_args()

    run_experiment(
        transactions_path=args.transactions,
        output_dir=args.output_dir,
        train_fraction=args.train_fraction,
        n_future_windows=args.windows,
        n_estimators=args.trees,
        explanation_samples=args.samples,
        random_state=args.random_state,
        reference_date=args.reference_date,
    )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
=== FILE: ./src/efd/experiments/exiffi_static_if.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd

from efd.data.preprocessing import (
    FeaturePolicy,
    NumericTransactionPreprocessor,
)
from efd.data.temporal import chronological_windows
from efd.models.isolation_forest import IsolationForestDetector


def load_baseline_1_artifacts(
    transactions_path: str | Path,
    train_fraction: float = 0.60,
    n_future_windows: int = 4,
    n_estimators: int = 300,
    contamination: str | float = "auto",
    random_state: int = 42,
) -> tuple[IsolationForestDetector, pd.DataFrame, list[str]]:
    """
    Load the exact Baseline 1 model and feature matrix.

    This reuses the same chronological split, preprocessing, and
    Isolation Forest fitting path as the Baseline 1 implementation.
    """

    df = pd.read_csv(
        transactions_path,
        low_memory=False,
    )

    train, _ = chronological_windows(
        df,
        time_col="TransactionDT",
        train_fraction=train_fraction,
        n_future_windows=n_future_windows,
    )

    preprocessor = NumericTransactionPreprocessor(
        policy=FeaturePolicy(
            target="isFraud",
            temporal="TransactionDT",
            identifier="TransactionID",
            max_missing_rate=0.95,
        )
    )

    X = preprocessor.fit_transform(train)
    feature_names = preprocessor.feature_names_

    if contamination == "auto":
        contamination_value: str | float = "auto"
    else:
        contamination_value = float(contamination)

    model = IsolationForestDetector(
        n_estimators=n_estimators,
        max_samples="auto",
        contamination=contamination_value,
        random_state=random_state,
        n_jobs=-1,
    )
    model.fit(X)

    return model, X, feature_names

=== FILE: ./src/efd/experiments/exiffi_smoke.py ===
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

from efd.experiments.exiffi_static_if import (
    load_baseline_1_artifacts,
)
from efd.explanations.exiffi import ExIFFI


model, X, feature_names = load_baseline_1_artifacts(
    transactions_path=(
        "data/raw/ieee_cis/train_transaction.csv"
    ),
    train_fraction=0.60,
    n_future_windows=4,
    n_estimators=300,
    contamination="auto",
    random_state=42,
)


explainer = ExIFFI(
    model=model,
    feature_names=feature_names,
)

X_sample = X[:100]

explanations = explainer.explain_batch(
    X_sample
)

rows = []

for explanation in explanations:

    importance = explanation.normalized

    ranking = np.argsort(
        -importance
    )[:10]

    row = {
        "sample_index":
            explanation.sample_index,

        "anomaly_score":
            explanation.anomaly_score,
    }

    for rank, feature_idx in enumerate(
        ranking,
        start=1,
    ):

        row[f"rank_{rank}_feature"] = (
            feature_names[feature_idx]
        )

        row[f"rank_{rank}_importance"] = (
            importance[feature_idx]
        )

    rows.append(row)


output = pd.DataFrame(rows)

output.to_csv(
    "results/tables/exiffi_smoke.csv",
    index=False,
)

print(output.head())

=== FILE: ./src/efd/experiments/static_if.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd

from efd.data.preprocessing import (
    FeaturePolicy,
    NumericTransactionPreprocessor,
)

from efd.data.temporal import (
    chronological_windows,
    transaction_date,
)

from efd.evaluation.detection import (
    evaluate_detection,
)

from efd.models.isolation_forest import (
    IsolationForestDetector,
)


def run_experiment(
    transactions_path: str,
    output_path: str,
    train_fraction: float = 0.60,
    n_future_windows: int = 4,
    n_estimators: int = 300,
    contamination: str | float = "auto",
    random_state: int = 42,
    reference_date: str = "2017-12-01",
):

    print("=" * 70)
    print("BASELINE 1 — STATIC ISOLATION FOREST")
    print("=" * 70)

    # 1. Load labelled IEEE-CIS transactions
    print("\n[1/6] Loading transaction dataset...")

    df = pd.read_csv(
        transactions_path,
        low_memory=False,
    )

    print(
        f"Loaded {len(df):,} transactions "
        f"and {len(df.columns):,} columns."
    )


    # 2. Chronological split
    print("\n[2/6] Creating chronological split...")

    train, future_windows = chronological_windows(
        df,
        time_col="TransactionDT",
        train_fraction=train_fraction,
        n_future_windows=n_future_windows,
    )

    print(
        f"Training observations: "
        f"{len(train):,}"
    )

    print(
        f"Future observations: "
        f"{sum(len(w) for w in future_windows):,}"
    )

    print(
        f"Future windows: "
        f"{len(future_windows)}"
    )

    print(
        "\nTraining temporal range:"
    )

    print(
        f"  {transaction_date(train['TransactionDT'].min(), reference_date)}"
        f" → "
        f"{transaction_date(train['TransactionDT'].max(), reference_date)}"
    )


    # 3. Fit preprocessing on TRAIN ONLY
    print(
        "\n[3/6] Fitting preprocessing "
        "on training period only..."
    )

    preprocessor = NumericTransactionPreprocessor(
        policy=FeaturePolicy(
            target="isFraud",
            temporal="TransactionDT",
            identifier="TransactionID",
            max_missing_rate=0.95,
        )
    )

    x_train = (
        preprocessor
        .fit_transform(train)
    )

    feature_names = (
        preprocessor
        .feature_names_
    )

    print(
        f"Selected features: "
        f"{len(feature_names)}"
    )

    print(
        f"Removed >95% missing: "
        f"{len(preprocessor.removed_high_missing_)}"
    )

    print(
        f"Removed constants: "
        f"{len(preprocessor.removed_constant_)}"
    )


    # 4. Train STATIC Isolation Forest
    print(
        "\n[4/6] Training static Isolation Forest..."
    )

    if contamination == "auto":
        contamination_value = "auto"
    else:
        contamination_value = float(
            contamination
        )

    detector = IsolationForestDetector(
        n_estimators=n_estimators,
        max_samples="auto",
        contamination=contamination_value,
        random_state=random_state,
        n_jobs=-1,
    )

    detector.fit(
        x_train
    )

    print(
        f"Isolation Forest trained with "
        f"{n_estimators} trees."
    )


    # 5. Evaluate future windows
    print(
        "\n[5/6] Evaluating future windows..."
    )

    results = []

    for window_number, window in enumerate(
        future_windows,
        start=1,
    ):

        x_window = (
            preprocessor
            .transform(window)
        )

        scores = (
            detector
            .anomaly_score(x_window)
        )

        predictions = (
            detector
            .predict_anomaly(x_window)
        )

        metrics = evaluate_detection(
            y_true=window["isFraud"],
            anomaly_scores=scores,
            predictions=predictions,
        )

        metrics.update(
            {
                "window": window_number,

                "date_start": transaction_date(
                    window["TransactionDT"].min(),
                    reference_date,
                ),

                "date_end": transaction_date(
                    window["TransactionDT"].max(),
                    reference_date,
                ),

                "n_features": len(
                    feature_names
                ),

                "n_estimators": (
                    n_estimators
                ),

                "contamination": str(
                    contamination_value
                ),

                "random_state": (
                    random_state
                ),

                "model": (
                    "static_isolation_forest"
                ),
            }
        )

        results.append(
            metrics
        )

        print(
            f"\nWindow {window_number}"
        )

        print(
            f"  N:        "
            f"{metrics['n']:,}"
        )

        print(
            f"  Fraud:    "
            f"{metrics['fraud']:,}"
        )

        print(
            f"  Fraud rate:"
            f" {metrics['fraud_rate']:.4%}"
        )

        print(
            f"  F1:       "
            f"{metrics['f1']:.6f}"
        )

        print(
            f"  PR-AUC:   "
            f"{metrics['pr_auc']:.6f}"
        )

    # 6. Save results
    print(
        "\n[6/6] Saving results..."
    )

    result_df = pd.DataFrame(
        results
    )

    output = Path(
        output_path
    )

    output.parent.mkdir(
        parents=True,
        exist_ok=True,
    )

    result_df.to_csv(
        output,
        index=False,
    )

    # Save exact feature list.
    feature_path = (
        output.parent
        / f"{output.stem}_features.txt"
    )

    feature_path.write_text(
        "\n".join(feature_names),
        encoding="utf-8",
    )

    # Save preprocessing audit.
    audit_path = (
        output.parent
        / f"{output.stem}_preprocessing.txt"
    )

    audit_lines = [
        "BASELINE 1 PREPROCESSING",
        "",
        f"Selected features: {len(feature_names)}",
        (
            "Maximum missing rate: "
            "0.95"
        ),
        (
            "Removed high-missing features: "
            f"{len(preprocessor.removed_high_missing_)}"
        ),
        (
            "Removed constants: "
            f"{len(preprocessor.removed_constant_)}"
        ),
        "",
        "High-missing features:",
        *preprocessor.removed_high_missing_,
        "",
        "Constant features:",
        *preprocessor.removed_constant_,
    ]

    audit_path.write_text(
        "\n".join(audit_lines),
        encoding="utf-8",
    )

    print(
        f"\nResults saved to:"
        f"\n{output}"
    )

    print(
        f"\nFeatures saved to:"
        f"\n{feature_path}"
    )

    print(
        f"\nPreprocessing audit saved to:"
        f"\n{audit_path}"
    )

    print("\n")
    print(result_df.to_string(index=False))

    return result_df


def main():

    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description=(
            "Baseline 1: "
            "Static Isolation Forest "
            "on IEEE-CIS"
        )
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--transactions",
        required=True,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--output",
        default=(
            "results/tables/"
            "static_if_v1.csv"
        ),
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--train-fraction",
        type=float,
        default=0.60,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--windows",
        type=int,
        default=4,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--trees",
        type=int,
        default=300,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--contamination",
        default="auto",
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--random-state",
        type=int,
        default=42,
    )

    parser.add_argument(
        "--reference-date",
        default="2017-12-01",
        help=(
            "Calendar date corresponding to TransactionDT=0 "
            "(default: 2017-12-01)."
        ),
    )

    args = parser.parse_args()

    run_experiment(
        transactions_path=(
            args.transactions
        ),
        output_path=(
            args.output
        ),
        train_fraction=(
            args.train_fraction
        ),
        n_future_windows=(
            args.windows
        ),
        n_estimators=(
            args.trees
        ),
        contamination=(
            args.contamination
        ),
        random_state=(
            args.random_state
        ),

    )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

"""Model implementations."""

=== FILE: ./src/efd/models/isolation_forest.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from sklearn.ensemble import IsolationForest


class IsolationForestDetector:
    """
    Isolation Forest wrapper.

    Important score convention:

        sklearn.score_samples()
            larger = more normal

        anomaly_score()
            larger = more anomalous

    Therefore:

        anomaly_score(x)
            = -score_samples(x)
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        n_estimators: int = 300,
        max_samples: str | int = "auto",
        contamination: str | float = "auto",
        random_state: int = 42,
        n_jobs: int = -1,
    ):

        self.model = IsolationForest(
            n_estimators=n_estimators,
            max_samples=max_samples,
            contamination=contamination,
            random_state=random_state,
            n_jobs=n_jobs,
        )
        self.feature_names_: list[str] | None = None

    def _as_model_frame(self, x):

        if self.feature_names_ is None:
            return x

        if isinstance(x, pd.DataFrame):
            return x

        x_array = np.asarray(x)

        if x_array.ndim == 1:
            x_array = x_array.reshape(1, -1)

        return pd.DataFrame(
            x_array,
            columns=self.feature_names_,
        )

    def fit(
        self,
        x,
    ) -> "IsolationForestDetector":

        if hasattr(x, "columns"):
            self.feature_names_ = list(x.columns)

        self.model.fit(x)

        return self

    def anomaly_score(
        self,
        x,
    ) -> np.ndarray:

        x_model = self._as_model_frame(x)

        return -self.model.score_samples(x_model)

    def predict_anomaly(
        self,
        x,
    ) -> np.ndarray:

        x_model = self._as_model_frame(x)

        sklearn_prediction = (
            self.model.predict(x_model)
        )

        # sklearn:
        #
        # +1 = normal
        # -1 = anomaly
        #
        # Research convention:
        #
        # 0 = normal
        # 1 = anomaly

        return (
            sklearn_prediction == -1
        ).astype(int)

=== FILE: ./src/efd/evaluation/explanation_drift.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy.spatial.distance import jensenshannon
from scipy.stats import spearmanr


def _validate_matrix(X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    X = np.asarray(X, dtype=float)

    if X.ndim != 2:
        raise ValueError(
            "Importance matrix must be 2-dimensional."
        )

    if X.shape[0] == 0 or X.shape[1] == 0:
        raise ValueError(
            "Importance matrix must not be empty."
        )

    if not np.isfinite(X).all():
        raise ValueError(
            "Importance matrix contains non-finite values."
        )

    return X


def _mean_distribution(
    X: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:

    X = _validate_matrix(X)

    # ExIFFI importance values are treated as
    # non-negative contribution magnitudes for
    # distributional comparison.
    X = np.clip(X, 0.0, None)

    distribution = X.mean(axis=0)

    total = distribution.sum()

    if total <= 0:
        return np.full(
            X.shape[1],
            1.0 / X.shape[1],
        )

    return distribution / total


def cosine_distance(
    X_a: np.ndarray,
    X_b: np.ndarray,
) -> float:

    a = _mean_distribution(X_a)
    b = _mean_distribution(X_b)

    if a.shape != b.shape:
        raise ValueError(
            "Matrices must have the same number of features."
        )

    denominator = (
        np.linalg.norm(a)
        * np.linalg.norm(b)
    )

    if denominator == 0:
        return 0.0

    similarity = (
        np.dot(a, b)
        / denominator
    )

    if np.isclose(
        similarity,
        1.0,
    ):
        return 0.0

    return float(
        1.0 - similarity
    )


def l1_distance(
    X_a: np.ndarray,
    X_b: np.ndarray,
) -> float:

    a = _mean_distribution(X_a)
    b = _mean_distribution(X_b)

    if a.shape != b.shape:
        raise ValueError(
            "Matrices must have the same number of features."
        )

    return float(
        np.abs(a - b).sum()
    )


def l2_distance(
    X_a: np.ndarray,
    X_b: np.ndarray,
) -> float:

    a = _mean_distribution(X_a)
    b = _mean_distribution(X_b)

    if a.shape != b.shape:
        raise ValueError(
            "Matrices must have the same number of features."
        )

    return float(
        np.linalg.norm(a - b)
    )


def js_divergence(
    X_a: np.ndarray,
    X_b: np.ndarray,
) -> float:

    a = _mean_distribution(X_a)
    b = _mean_distribution(X_b)

    if a.shape != b.shape:
        raise ValueError(
            "Matrices must have the same number of features."
        )

    return float(
        jensenshannon(
            a,
            b,
            base=2.0,
        )
        ** 2
    )


def rank_correlation(
    X_a: np.ndarray,
    X_b: np.ndarray,
) -> float:

    a = _mean_distribution(X_a)
    b = _mean_distribution(X_b)

    if a.shape != b.shape:
        raise ValueError(
            "Matrices must have the same number of features."
        )

    result = spearmanr(
        a,
        b,
    )

    statistic = result.statistic

    if not np.isfinite(statistic):
        return 0.0

    return float(statistic)


def top_k_features(
    X: np.ndarray,
    k: int,
) -> set[int]:

    X = _validate_matrix(X)

    if k <= 0:
        raise ValueError(
            "k must be positive."
        )

    k = min(
        k,
        X.shape[1],
    )

    mean_importance = X.mean(
        axis=0
    )

    indices = np.argsort(
        -mean_importance
    )[:k]

    return set(
        indices.tolist()
    )


def top_k_jaccard(
    X_a: np.ndarray,
    X_b: np.ndarray,
    k: int,
) -> float:

    features_a = top_k_features(
        X_a,
        k,
    )

    features_b = top_k_features(
        X_b,
        k,
    )

    union = (
        features_a
        | features_b
    )

    if not union:
        return 1.0

    intersection = (
        features_a
        & features_b
    )

    return float(
        len(intersection)
        / len(union)
    )


def feature_drift_table(
    X_a: np.ndarray,
    X_b: np.ndarray,
    feature_names: list[str],
    label_a: str,
    label_b: str,
) -> pd.DataFrame:

    X_a = _validate_matrix(X_a)
    X_b = _validate_matrix(X_b)

    if X_a.shape[1] != X_b.shape[1]:
        raise ValueError(
            "Matrices must have the same number of features."
        )

    if len(feature_names) != X_a.shape[1]:
        raise ValueError(
            "feature_names length does not match matrix width."
        )

    distribution_a = _mean_distribution(
        X_a
    )

    distribution_b = _mean_distribution(
        X_b
    )

    rank_a = (
        pd.Series(distribution_a)
        .rank(
            method="average",
            ascending=False,
        )
        .to_numpy()
    )

    rank_b = (
        pd.Series(distribution_b)
        .rank(
            method="average",
            ascending=False,
        )
        .to_numpy()
    )

    result = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "feature": feature_names,

            f"mean_importance_{label_a}":
                distribution_a,

            f"mean_importance_{label_b}":
                distribution_b,

            "importance_change":
                distribution_b
                - distribution_a,

            f"rank_{label_a}":
                rank_a.astype(int),

            f"rank_{label_b}":
                rank_b.astype(int),

            "rank_change":
                rank_b - rank_a,

            "absolute_importance_change":
                np.abs(
                    distribution_b
                    - distribution_a
                ),
        }
    )

    return (
        result
        .sort_values(
            "absolute_importance_change",
            ascending=False,
        )
        .reset_index(drop=True)
    )


def explanation_drift_summary(
    X_a: np.ndarray,
    X_b: np.ndarray,
    label_a: str,
    label_b: str,
) -> dict:

    return {
        "comparison":
            f"{label_a}_{label_b}",

        "cosine_distance":
            cosine_distance(
                X_a,
                X_b,
            ),

        "js_divergence":
            js_divergence(
                X_a,
                X_b,
            ),

        "l1_distance":
            l1_distance(
                X_a,
                X_b,
            ),

        "l2_distance":
            l2_distance(
                X_a,
                X_b,
            ),

        "spearman_rank_correlation":
            rank_correlation(
                X_a,
                X_b,
            ),
    }
=== FILE: ./src/efd/evaluation/detection.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

from sklearn.metrics import (
    average_precision_score,
    f1_score,
)


def evaluate_detection(
    y_true,
    anomaly_scores,
    predictions,
) -> dict:

    y_true = np.asarray(
        y_true
    )

    anomaly_scores = np.asarray(
        anomaly_scores
    )

    predictions = np.asarray(
        predictions
    )

    if len(y_true) == 0:
        raise ValueError(
            "Cannot evaluate an empty window."
        )

    result = {
        "n": int(
            len(y_true)
        ),

        "fraud": int(
            y_true.sum()
        ),

        "fraud_rate": float(
            y_true.mean()
        ),

        "f1": float(
            f1_score(
                y_true,
                predictions,
                zero_division=0,
            )
        ),

        "pr_auc": float(
            average_precision_score(
                y_true,
                anomaly_scores,
            )
        ),
    }

    return result
=== FILE: ./src/efd/evaluation/__init__.py ===
"""Evaluation utilities."""

=== FILE: ./src/efd/evaluation/explanation_fidelity.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def score_samples_with_feature_names(
    model,
    x: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """Score arrays using names recorded when the model was fitted."""

    x_array = np.asarray(
        x,
        dtype=float,
    )

    if hasattr(model, "feature_names_in_"):
        if x_array.ndim == 1:
            x_array = x_array.reshape(1, -1)

        x_input = pd.DataFrame(
            x_array,
            columns=model.feature_names_in_,
        )
    else:
        x_input = x_array

    return model.score_samples(x_input)


@dataclass
class FidelityResult:
    """
    Fidelity evaluation for one observation.

    The score measures how much the model output changes
    when features identified by the explanation are perturbed.
    """

    sample_index: int

    baseline_score: float

    top_k: int

    top_k_mass: float

    perturbed_score: float

    score_change: float

    relative_score_change: float


def perturb_features(
    x: np.ndarray,
    feature_indices: np.ndarray,
    baseline: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Replace selected features with baseline values.

    Parameters
    ----------
    x:
        One observation.

    feature_indices:
        Features to perturb.

    baseline:
        Baseline feature values.

    Returns
    -------
    Perturbed observation.
    """

    x_perturbed = np.asarray(
        x,
        dtype=float,
    ).copy()

    x_perturbed[
        feature_indices
    ] = baseline[
        feature_indices
    ]

    return x_perturbed


def evaluate_local_fidelity(
    model,
    x: np.ndarray,
    explanation,
    baseline: np.ndarray,
    k: int,
) -> FidelityResult:
    """
    Evaluate whether the top-k ExIFFI features actually
    influence the model anomaly score.

    The original observation is scored first.

    Then the top-k explanation features are replaced
    with baseline values.

    Fidelity is measured as the resulting change
    in model score.
    """

    x = np.asarray(
        x,
        dtype=float,
    )

    baseline = np.asarray(
        baseline,
        dtype=float,
    )

    normalized = explanation.normalized

    ranked_indices = np.argsort(
        -normalized
    )

    top_indices = ranked_indices[:k]

    top_k_mass = float(
        normalized[top_indices].sum()
    )

    baseline_score = float(
        score_samples_with_feature_names(
            model,
            x.reshape(1, -1),
        )[0]
    )

    x_perturbed = perturb_features(
        x=x,
        feature_indices=top_indices,
        baseline=baseline,
    )

    perturbed_score = float(
        score_samples_with_feature_names(
            model,
            x_perturbed.reshape(1, -1),
        )[0]
    )

    score_change = (
        perturbed_score
        - baseline_score
    )

    denominator = max(
        abs(baseline_score),
        1e-12,
    )

    relative_score_change = (
        abs(score_change)
        / denominator
    )

    return FidelityResult(
        sample_index=explanation.sample_index,
        baseline_score=baseline_score,
        top_k=k,
        top_k_mass=top_k_mass,
        perturbed_score=perturbed_score,
        score_change=score_change,
        relative_score_change=relative_score_change,
    )


def evaluate_fidelity_matrix(
    model,
    X: np.ndarray,
    explanations,
    baseline: np.ndarray,
    k_values: list[int],
) -> list[FidelityResult]:
    """
    Evaluate local fidelity across observations and
    multiple explanation sizes.
    """

    X = np.asarray(
        X,
        dtype=float,
    )

    results = []

    for explanation in explanations:

        sample_index = (
            explanation.sample_index
        )

        x = X[sample_index]

        for k in k_values:

            result = evaluate_local_fidelity(
                model=model,
                x=x,
                explanation=explanation,
                baseline=baseline,
                k=k,
            )

            results.append(result)

    return results

def evaluate_random_fidelity_matrix(
    model,
    X: np.ndarray,
    explanations,
    baseline: np.ndarray,
    k_values: list[int],
    random_state: int = 42,
):
    """
    Evaluate model-score change when randomly selected
    features are replaced by the training baseline.

    This provides a control condition for ExIFFI local
    fidelity.

    The perturbation procedure is identical to ExIFFI
    fidelity; only the feature-selection mechanism differs.
    """

    X = np.asarray(
        X,
        dtype=float,
    )

    baseline = np.asarray(
        baseline,
        dtype=float,
    )

    if X.ndim != 2:
        raise ValueError("X must be two-dimensional.")

    if baseline.shape != (X.shape[1],):
        raise ValueError(
            "baseline must contain one value per feature."
        )

    rng = np.random.default_rng(random_state)

    n_features = X.shape[1]

    results = []

    for explanation, x in zip(
        explanations,
        X,
    ):

        baseline_score = float(
            score_samples_with_feature_names(
                model,
                x.reshape(1, -1),
            )[0]
        )

        for k in k_values:

            if not 0 < k <= n_features:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"k must be between 1 and the number "
                    f"of features ({n_features})."
                )

            random_features = rng.choice(
                n_features,
                size=k,
                replace=False,
            )

            perturbed = x.copy()

            perturbed[
                random_features
            ] = baseline[
                random_features
            ]

            perturbed_score = float(
                score_samples_with_feature_names(
                    model,
                    perturbed.reshape(1, -1),
                )[0]
            )

            score_change = (
                perturbed_score
                - baseline_score
            )

            denominator = max(
                abs(baseline_score),
                1e-12,
            )

            relative_score_change = (
                abs(score_change)
                / denominator
            )

            results.append(
                {
                    "sample_index": (
                        explanation.sample_index
                    ),
                    "top_k": k,
                    "baseline_score": (
                        baseline_score
                    ),
                    "perturbed_score": (
                        perturbed_score
                    ),
                    "score_change": (
                        score_change
                    ),
                    "relative_score_change": (
                        relative_score_change
                    ),
                }
            )

    return pd.DataFrame(results)

=== FILE: ./src/efd/evaluation/explanation.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Iterable

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def _normalize_rows(values: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Normalize each row so that its values sum to 1.
    """

    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=float)

    if values.ndim != 2:
        raise ValueError("values must be two-dimensional.")

    totals = values.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)

    return np.divide(
        values,
        totals,
        out=np.zeros_like(values),
        where=totals > 0,
    )


def top_k_mass(
    importance: np.ndarray,
    k: int,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Return the fraction of total explanation importance
    contained in the top-k features for each observation.
    """

    importance = _normalize_rows(importance)

    if k <= 0:
        raise ValueError("k must be positive.")

    k = min(k, importance.shape[1])

    sorted_values = np.sort(
        importance,
        axis=1,
    )[:, ::-1]

    return sorted_values[:, :k].sum(axis=1)


def explanation_entropy(
    importance: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Shannon entropy of each explanation.

    Importance values are normalized before computing entropy.
    Zero-valued features contribute zero.
    """

    importance = _normalize_rows(importance)

    safe = np.where(
        importance > 0,
        importance,
        1.0,
    )

    return -np.sum(
        np.where(
            importance > 0,
            importance * np.log(safe),
            0.0,
        ),
        axis=1,
    )


def effective_features(
    importance: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Effective number of features represented by an explanation.

    Defined as:

        exp(H)

    where H is Shannon entropy.
    """

    return np.exp(
        explanation_entropy(importance)
    )


def nonzero_feature_count(
    importance: np.ndarray,
    tolerance: float = 0.0,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Number of features with importance greater than tolerance.
    """

    importance = np.asarray(
        importance,
        dtype=float,
    )

    return np.sum(
        importance > tolerance,
        axis=1,
    )


def cosine_similarity(
    a: np.ndarray,
    b: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Row-wise cosine similarity between two explanation matrices.
    """

    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=float)

    if a.shape != b.shape:
        raise ValueError(
            "a and b must have identical shapes."
        )

    numerator = np.sum(
        a * b,
        axis=1,
    )

    denominator = (
        np.linalg.norm(a, axis=1)
        * np.linalg.norm(b, axis=1)
    )

    return np.divide(
        numerator,
        denominator,
        out=np.zeros_like(numerator),
        where=denominator > 0,
    )


def top_k_feature_set(
    importance: np.ndarray,
    feature_names: Iterable[str],
    k: int,
) -> list[set[str]]:
    """
    Return the top-k feature set for every observation.
    """

    importance = np.asarray(
        importance,
        dtype=float,
    )

    feature_names = list(feature_names)

    if importance.shape[1] != len(feature_names):
        raise ValueError(
            "Number of features does not match "
            "feature_names."
        )

    k = min(k, importance.shape[1])

    result = []

    for row in importance:

        indices = np.argsort(
            -row
        )[:k]

        result.append(
            {
                feature_names[i]
                for i in indices
            }
        )

    return result


def top_k_overlap(
    importance_a: np.ndarray,
    importance_b: np.ndarray,
    feature_names: Iterable[str],
    k: int = 10,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Jaccard overlap between the top-k features of
    two explanations.

        |A ∩ B|
        -------
        |A ∪ B|
    """

    sets_a = top_k_feature_set(
        importance_a,
        feature_names,
        k,
    )

    sets_b = top_k_feature_set(
        importance_b,
        feature_names,
        k,
    )

    overlaps = []

    for a, b in zip(sets_a, sets_b):

        union = a | b

        if not union:
            overlaps.append(1.0)
            continue

        overlaps.append(
            len(a & b) / len(union)
        )

    return np.asarray(
        overlaps,
        dtype=float,
    )


def evaluate_explanation_matrix(
    importance: np.ndarray,
    feature_names: Iterable[str],
    window: int | None = None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Compute descriptive explanation metrics for a batch
    of local explanations.

    This function intentionally does not claim that these
    metrics measure causal or decision fidelity.

    They characterize explanation concentration,
    sparsity, and structure.
    """

    importance = np.asarray(
        importance,
        dtype=float,
    )

    if importance.ndim != 2:
        raise ValueError(
            "importance must be two-dimensional."
        )

    feature_names = list(feature_names)

    if importance.shape[1] != len(feature_names):
        raise ValueError(
            "Number of features does not match "
            "feature_names."
        )

    normalized = _normalize_rows(
        importance
    )

    result = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "sample_index": np.arange(
                len(normalized)
            ),
            "top_1_mass": top_k_mass(
                normalized,
                1,
            ),
            "top_5_mass": top_k_mass(
                normalized,
                5,
            ),
            "top_10_mass": top_k_mass(
                normalized,
                10,
            ),
            "entropy": explanation_entropy(
                normalized
            ),
            "effective_features": effective_features(
                normalized
            ),
            "nonzero_features": nonzero_feature_count(
                normalized
            ),
        }
    )

    if window is not None:
        result.insert(
            0,
            "window",
            window,
        )

    return result


def summarize_explanation_metrics(
    metrics: pd.DataFrame,
) -> pd.Series:
    """
    Produce mean/median summary statistics for a batch
    of explanation metrics.
    """

    required = [
        "top_1_mass",
        "top_5_mass",
        "top_10_mass",
        "entropy",
        "effective_features",
        "nonzero_features",
    ]

    missing = [
        column
        for column in required
        if column not in metrics.columns
    ]

    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Missing required columns: {missing}"
        )

    return pd.Series(
        {
            "n_samples": len(metrics),

            "mean_top_1_mass":
                metrics["top_1_mass"].mean(),

            "mean_top_5_mass":
                metrics["top_5_mass"].mean(),

            "mean_top_10_mass":
                metrics["top_10_mass"].mean(),

            "mean_entropy":
                metrics["entropy"].mean(),

            "mean_effective_features":
                metrics["effective_features"].mean(),

            "mean_nonzero_features":
                metrics["nonzero_features"].mean(),

            "median_top_1_mass":
                metrics["top_1_mass"].median(),

            "median_top_5_mass":
                metrics["top_5_mass"].median(),

            "median_top_10_mass":
                metrics["top_10_mass"].median(),

            "median_entropy":
                metrics["entropy"].median(),

            "median_effective_features":
                metrics["effective_features"].median(),

            "median_nonzero_features":
                metrics["nonzero_features"].median(),
        }
    )
=== FILE: ./src/efd/data/temporal.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def transaction_date(
    transaction_dt: float,
    reference_date: str | pd.Timestamp,
) -> str:
    """Return the ISO calendar date for a TransactionDT offset in seconds."""
    timestamp = pd.Timestamp(reference_date) + pd.to_timedelta(
        transaction_dt,
        unit="s",
    )
    return timestamp.date().isoformat()


def chronological_windows(
    frame: pd.DataFrame,
    time_col: str = "TransactionDT",
    train_fraction: float = 0.60,
    n_future_windows: int = 4,
) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, list[pd.DataFrame]]:
    """
    Chronologically split the dataset.

    Parameters
    ----------
    frame:
        Complete labelled transaction dataframe.

    time_col:
        Temporal ordering variable.

    train_fraction:
        Fraction of earliest observations used for training.

    n_future_windows:
        Number of chronological evaluation windows.

    Returns
    -------
    train:
        Earliest chronological training observations.

    future_windows:
        Sequential future evaluation windows.
    """

    if not 0 < train_fraction < 1:
        raise ValueError(
            "train_fraction must be between 0 and 1."
        )

    if n_future_windows < 1:
        raise ValueError(
            "n_future_windows must be >= 1."
        )

    if time_col not in frame.columns:
        raise KeyError(
            f"Temporal column '{time_col}' not found."
        )

    # Stable sort is important for reproducibility if equal timestamps occur.
    ordered = (
        frame
        .sort_values(
            time_col,
            kind="mergesort"
        )
        .reset_index(drop=True)
    )

    split_idx = int(
        len(ordered) * train_fraction
    )

    train = ordered.iloc[:split_idx].copy()

    future = ordered.iloc[split_idx:].copy()

    # Equal-count windows.
    window_indices = np.array_split(
        np.arange(len(future)),
        n_future_windows,
    )

    future_windows = [
        future.iloc[indices].copy()
        for indices in window_indices
        if len(indices) > 0
    ]

    return train, future_windows

=== FILE: ./src/efd/data/audit.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

TARGET = "isFraud"
TIME = "TransactionDT"
ID = "TransactionID"


def transaction_schema(path: str | Path, sample_rows: int = 10_000) -> pd.DataFrame:
    df = pd.read_csv(path, nrows=sample_rows, low_memory=False)
    rows = []
    for c in df.columns:
        s = df[c]
        rows.append({
            "feature": c,
            "dtype": str(s.dtype),
            "sample_non_null": int(s.notna().sum()),
            "sample_missing_rate": float(s.isna().mean()),
            "n_unique_sample": int(s.nunique(dropna=True)),
            "is_numeric": bool(pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(s)),
        })
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)


def scan_transactions(path: str | Path, chunksize: int = 100_000, time_bins: int = 20) -> dict:
    path = Path(path)
    total_rows = 0
    fraud_count = 0
    dt_min = None
    dt_max = None
    missing_counts = None
    dtypes = None
    bin_rows = np.zeros(time_bins, dtype=np.int64)
    bin_fraud = np.zeros(time_bins, dtype=np.int64)

    for chunk in pd.read_csv(path, chunksize=chunksize, low_memory=False):
        if dtypes is None:
            dtypes = {c: str(t) for c, t in chunk.dtypes.items()}
            missing_counts = pd.Series(0, index=chunk.columns, dtype="int64")
        total_rows += len(chunk)
        fraud_count += int(chunk[TARGET].fillna(0).sum())
        dt = pd.to_numeric(chunk[TIME], errors="coerce")
        cmin, cmax = dt.min(), dt.max()
        if pd.notna(cmin): dt_min = cmin if dt_min is None else min(dt_min, cmin)
        if pd.notna(cmax): dt_max = cmax if dt_max is None else max(dt_max, cmax)
        missing_counts = missing_counts.add(chunk.isna().sum(), fill_value=0)

    if dt_min is not None and dt_max is not None and dt_max > dt_min:
        edges = np.linspace(dt_min, dt_max, time_bins + 1)
        for chunk in pd.read_csv(path, chunksize=chunksize, low_memory=False):
            dt = pd.to_numeric(chunk[TIME], errors="coerce").to_numpy()
            idx = np.searchsorted(edges, dt, side="right") - 1
            idx = np.clip(idx, 0, time_bins - 1)
            valid = np.isfinite(dt)
            np.add.at(bin_rows, idx[valid], 1)
            fraud = chunk[TARGET].fillna(0).to_numpy()
            np.add.at(bin_fraud, idx[valid], fraud[valid].astype(np.int64))

    temporal = []
    if dt_min is not None:
        edges = np.linspace(dt_min, dt_max, time_bins + 1)
        for i in range(time_bins):
            temporal.append({
                "window": i,
                "dt_start": float(edges[i]),
                "dt_end": float(edges[i + 1]),
                "rows": int(bin_rows[i]),
                "fraud": int(bin_fraud[i]),
                "fraud_rate": float(bin_fraud[i] / bin_rows[i]) if bin_rows[i] else None,
            })

    missingness = [
        {"feature": k, "missing": int(v), "missing_rate": float(v / total_rows)}
        for k, v in missing_counts.sort_values(ascending=False).items()
    ]
    return {
        "file": str(path),
        "rows": total_rows,
        "columns": len(dtypes or {}),
        "fraud_count": fraud_count,
        "fraud_rate": fraud_count / total_rows if total_rows else None,
        "transaction_dt_min": None if dt_min is None else float(dt_min),
        "transaction_dt_max": None if dt_max is None else float(dt_max),
        "dtypes": dtypes or {},
        "missingness": missingness,
        "temporal_summary": temporal,
    }


def identity_join_coverage(transactions_path: str | Path, identity_path: str | Path, chunksize: int = 100_000) -> dict:
    identity_ids = set()
    for chunk in pd.read_csv(identity_path, usecols=[ID], chunksize=chunksize, low_memory=False):
        identity_ids.update(chunk[ID].dropna().astype("int64").tolist())
    total = matched = 0
    for chunk in pd.read_csv(transactions_path, usecols=[ID], chunksize=chunksize, low_memory=False):
        ids = chunk[ID].dropna().astype("int64")
        total += len(ids)
        matched += int(ids.isin(identity_ids).sum())
    return {
        "transaction_rows_considered": total,
        "identity_unique_transaction_ids": len(identity_ids),
        "matched_transaction_rows": matched,
        "matched_rate": matched / total if total else None,
        "unmatched_transaction_rows": total - matched,
        "join_type_for_modeling": "left",
    }


def write_audit(audit: dict, output_dir: str | Path) -> None:
    output_dir = Path(output_dir)
    output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    summary = {k: v for k, v in audit.items() if k not in {"missingness", "temporal_summary", "schema"}}
    (output_dir / "dataset_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
    pd.DataFrame(audit["missingness"]).to_csv(output_dir / "missingness.csv", index=False)
    pd.DataFrame(audit["temporal_summary"]).to_csv(output_dir / "temporal_summary.csv", index=False)
    audit["schema"].to_csv(output_dir / "feature_schema.csv", index=False)
    if "identity_join" in audit:
        (output_dir / "join_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(audit["identity_join"], indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

=== FILE: ./src/efd/data/preprocessing.py ===
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import pandas as pd
from sklearn.impute import SimpleImputer


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FeaturePolicy:
    """
    Defines the Baseline 1 feature policy.
    """

    target: str = "isFraud"

    temporal: str = "TransactionDT"

    identifier: str = "TransactionID"

    max_missing_rate: float = 0.95


class NumericTransactionPreprocessor:
    """
    Leakage-safe numeric feature preprocessing.

    Baseline 1:
        - numeric transaction features only
        - exclude target
        - exclude temporal variable
        - exclude identifier
        - remove high-missingness columns
        - remove training-period constants
        - median imputation

    Every decision is fitted on the chronological training period.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        policy: FeaturePolicy | None = None,
    ):

        self.policy = (
            policy
            if policy is not None
            else FeaturePolicy()
        )

        self.features_: list[str] | None = None

        self.imputer_: SimpleImputer | None = None

        self.feature_missing_rates_: dict[str, float] = {}

        self.removed_high_missing_: list[str] = []

        self.removed_constant_: list[str] = []

    def fit(
        self,
        train: pd.DataFrame,
    ) -> "NumericTransactionPreprocessor":

        excluded = {
            self.policy.target,
            self.policy.temporal,
            self.policy.identifier,
        }

        # Only numeric columns.
        numeric = train.select_dtypes(
            include=["number"]
        )

        candidates = [
            column
            for column in numeric.columns
            if column not in excluded
        ]

        if not candidates:
            raise ValueError(
                "No numeric candidate features found."
            )

        # Step 1: Missingness filtering
        missing_rates = (
            train[candidates]
            .isna()
            .mean()
        )

        self.feature_missing_rates_ = (
            missing_rates
            .to_dict()
        )

        self.removed_high_missing_ = [
            column
            for column in candidates
            if missing_rates[column]
            > self.policy.max_missing_rate
        ]

        candidates = [
            column
            for column in candidates
            if column not in self.removed_high_missing_
        ]

        # Step 2: Remove training-period constants
        cardinalities = (
            train[candidates]
            .nunique(
                dropna=True
            )
        )

        self.removed_constant_ = [
            column
            for column in candidates
            if cardinalities[column] <= 1
        ]

        candidates = [
            column
            for column in candidates
            if column not in self.removed_constant_
        ]

        if not candidates:
            raise ValueError(
                "No usable features remain after filtering."
            )

        self.features_ = candidates


        # Step 3: Fit imputer (ONLY on training period)
        self.imputer_ = SimpleImputer(
            strategy="median"
        )

        self.imputer_.fit(
            train[self.features_]
        )

        return self

    def transform(
        self,
        frame: pd.DataFrame,
    ) -> pd.DataFrame:

        if self.features_ is None:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "Preprocessor has not been fitted."
            )

        if self.imputer_ is None:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "Imputer has not been fitted."
            )

        # Explicitly recreate the frozen training schema.
        x = frame.reindex(
            columns=self.features_
        )

        transformed = self.imputer_.transform(
            x
        )

        return pd.DataFrame(
            transformed,
            columns=self.features_,
            index=frame.index,
        )

    def fit_transform(
        self,
        train: pd.DataFrame,
    ) -> pd.DataFrame:

        self.fit(train)

        return self.transform(train)

    @property
    def feature_names_(self) -> list[str]:

        if self.features_ is None:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "Preprocessor has not been fitted."
            )

        return list(self.features_)
